## Canada Shipping Act, 2001 {#canada-shipping-act-2001 .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 2001, c. 26
:::

Assented to 2001-11-01

An Act respecting shipping and navigation and to amend the Shipping
Conferences Exemption Act, 1987 and other Acts
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: Enacts
Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the Senate and House
of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-50746 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Canada Shipping Act, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15).

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-50749 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Act.

[[adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2001, c. 29, s.
    72\]]{.Repealed}

[[authorized representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[authorized representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a Canadian vessel, the person
      referred to in subsection 14(1);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a fleet registered under Part 2,
      the person referred to in subsection 75.03(5); and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a foreign vessel, the
      master. ([représentant autorisé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[bare-boat charter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[bare-boat charter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel charter
    agreement under which the charterer has complete possession and
    control of the vessel, including the right to appoint its master and
    crew. ([affrètement coque nue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Canadian maritime document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canadian maritime document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence,
    permit, certificate or other document that is issued by the Minister
    of Transport under Part 1 (General), 3 (Personnel), 4 (Safety), 9
    (Pollution Prevention --- Department of Transport) or 11
    (Enforcement --- Department of Transport) to verify that the person
    to whom or vessel to which it is issued has met requirements under
    that Part. ([document maritime canadien]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Canadian vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canadian vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is
    registered or listed under Part 2 (Registration, Listing and
    Recording) or that is exempted under the regulations from the
    registration requirement in subsection 46(1). ([bâtiment
    canadien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[foreign vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[foreign vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is not a
    Canadian vessel or a pleasure craft. ([bâtiment
    étranger]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[government vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[government vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is
    owned by and is in the service of Her Majesty in right of Canada or
    a province or that is in the exclusive possession of Her Majesty in
    that right. ([bâtiment d'État]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[gross tonnage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[gross tonnage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the volume of a vessel
    as determined by a tonnage measurer or calculated in accordance with
    the regulations made under paragraph 77(h). ([jauge
    brute]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hazardous and noxious substances handling facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hazardous and noxious substances handling
    facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a facility that is used or that
    will be used in the loading or unloading of hazardous and noxious
    substances to or from vessels. ([installation de manutention de
    substances nocives et potentiellement dangereuses]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[master]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[master]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the person in command and
    charge of a vessel. It does not include a licensed pilot, within the
    meaning of section 1.1 of the [Pilotage Act](/eng/acts/P-14), while
    the pilot is performing pilotage duties under that
    Act. ([capitaine]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[oil handling facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[oil handling facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a facility,
    including an oil terminal, that is used or that will be used in the
    loading or unloading of petroleum in any form, including crude oil,
    fuel oil, sludge, oil refuse and refined products, to or from
    vessels. ([installation de manutention
    d'hydrocarbures]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[passenger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[passenger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person carried on a vessel
    by the owner or operator, other than

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person carried on a Safety Convention vessel
      who is

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the master, a member of the crew or a person
        employed or engaged in any capacity on board the vessel on the
        business of that vessel, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} under one year of age;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person carried on a vessel that is not a Safety
      Convention vessel who is

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the master, a member of the crew or a person
        employed or engaged in any capacity on board the vessel on the
        business of that vessel, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a guest on board the vessel, if the vessel is
        used exclusively for pleasure and the guest is carried on it
        without remuneration or any object of profit;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person carried on a vessel in pursuance of the
      obligation on the master to carry shipwrecked, distressed or other
      persons or by reason of any circumstances that neither the master
      nor the owner could have prevented; or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person of a prescribed
      class. ([passager]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pleasure craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pleasure craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel that is used
    for pleasure and does not carry passengers, and includes a vessel of
    a prescribed class. ([embarcation de plaisance]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[port authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[port authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    subsection 2(1) of the [Canada Marine
    Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7). ([administration portuaire]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by regulations
    made by the Governor in Council. ([Version anglaise
    seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[qualified person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[qualified person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian citizen or a permanent resident within
      the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee
      Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5); or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a corporation incorporated under the laws of
      Canada or a province. ([personne qualifiée]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[Register]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Register]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Register of
    Vessels established under section 43. ([Registre]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Safety Convention vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Safety Convention vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vessel in
    respect of which the International Convention for the Safety of Life
    at Sea, listed in Schedule 1, applies. ([bâtiment assujetti à la
    Convention sur la sécurité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Transportation Appeal
    Tribunal of Canada established by subsection 2(1) of the
    [Transportation Appeal Tribunal of Canada
    Act](/eng/acts/T-18.5). ([Tribunal]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a boat, ship or craft designed,
    used or capable of being used solely or partly for navigation in,
    on, through or immediately above water, without regard to method or
    lack of propulsion, and includes such a vessel that is under
    construction. It does not include a floating object of a prescribed
    class. ([bâtiment]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[wages]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[wages]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes
    emoluments. ([gages]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Descriptive cross-references

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** If, in any provision of
this Act, a reference to another provision of this Act is followed by
words in parentheses that are or purport to be descriptive of the
subject-matter of the provision referred to, those words form no part of
the provision in which they occur but are inserted for convenience of
reference only.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, on the recommendation of the Minister of Transport, make
regulations prescribing anything that may be prescribed under section 2.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 4
- 2005, c. 29, s. 15
:::

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-50792 .Part}

### [Her Majesty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50793 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding on Her Majesty

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided, this Act is binding on Her Majesty in right of Canada or a
province.

### [Objectives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50796 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives of Act

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** The objectives of this Act
are to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} protect the health and well-being of individuals,
  including the crews of vessels, who participate in marine
  transportation and commerce;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} promote safety in marine transportation and
  recreational boating;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} protect the marine environment from damage due to
  navigation and shipping activities;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} develop a regulatory scheme that encourages viable,
  effective and economical marine transportation and commerce;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} promote an efficient marine transportation system;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} develop a regulatory scheme that encourages the
  viable, effective and economical use of Canadian waters by
  recreational boaters;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} ensure that Canada can meet its international
  obligations under bilateral and multilateral agreements with respect
  to navigation and shipping;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} encourage the harmonization of marine practices; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} establish an effective inspection and enforcement
  program.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50808 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision
  of this Act, this Act does not apply in respect of a vessel, facility
  or aircraft that belongs to the Canadian Forces or a foreign military
  force or in respect of any other vessel, facility or aircraft that is
  under the command, control or direction of the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister of Transport, make regulations varying or excluding the
  application, in respect of government vessels, of any provision of
  this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflicts with foreign rules

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under this Act do not, unless they
  expressly provide otherwise, apply in respect of a Canadian vessel in
  the waters of a country other than Canada if the regulations are
  inconsistent with a law of that country that, by its terms, applies in
  respect of the vessel when in the waters of that country.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of this Part

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies in
respect of Canadian vessels everywhere, in respect of foreign vessels in
Canadian waters, and in respect of pleasure craft that are not Canadian
vessels but that are in Canadian waters or in waters in the exclusive
economic zone of Canada. However

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} regulations made under paragraph 35(1)(d) in respect
  of pollution also apply, if they so state, in respect of foreign
  vessels in waters in the exclusive economic zone of Canada; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsections 10(2.1) and 35.1(1) also apply in respect
  of foreign vessels in waters in the exclusive economic zone of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 8
- 2018, c. 27, s. 688
- [2023, c. 26, s. 351](#1410259-1413655){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410259-1413655.html"
  role="button"}
:::

:::

### [Ministerial Responsibility]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50824 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Minister of Transport

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided in this Act, the Minister of Transport is responsible for the
administration of this Act.

### [Powers of Ministers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50827 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister of Transport or the Minister of Fisheries and Oceans may,
  with respect to that Minister's responsibilities under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish consultative bodies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issue bulletins, guidelines and standards; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} enter into agreements or arrangements respecting
    the administration or enforcement of any provision of this Act, the
    regulations or an interim order made under subsection 10.1(1) or
    (1.1) and authorize any person or organization --- including a
    provincial government, a local authority and a government, council
    or other entity authorized to act on behalf of an Indigenous group
    --- with whom or which an agreement or arrangement is entered into
    to exercise the powers or perform the duties and functions under
    this Act that are specified in the agreement or arrangement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption power of Ministers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport or the Minister of
  Fisheries and Oceans may, with respect to that Minister's
  responsibilities under this Act, exempt for a specified period any
  authorized representative, person, class of persons, master, vessel,
  class of vessels, operator of an oil handling facility, oil handling
  facility or class of oil handling facilities, operator of a hazardous
  and noxious substances handling facility, hazardous and noxious
  substances handling facility or class of hazardous and noxious
  substances handling facilities from the application of any provision
  of this Act, the regulations or an interim order made under subsection
  10.1(1) or (1.1), subject to any conditions that that Minister
  considers appropriate, if that Minister is of the opinion that the
  exemption is in the interest of preventing damage to property or the
  environment or is in the public interest or interest of public health
  or safety.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption power of Minister of
  Transport

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may, with respect to his
  or her responsibilities under this Act, exempt for a period of not
  more than three years any person or vessel or class of persons or
  vessels from the application of any provisions of this Act or the
  regulations, subject to any conditions that the Minister considers
  appropriate, if the exemption would allow the undertaking of research
  and development, including in respect of any type of vessels,
  technologies, systems, components or procedures and practices that
  may, in the Minister's opinion, enhance marine safety or environmental
  protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport shall publish, on the
  Department of Transport's Internet site or by any other means that the
  Minister of Transport considers appropriate, a notice of every
  exemption granted under subsection (2.1), as soon as feasible after it
  is granted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any conditions that the Minister of
  Transport considers appropriate, the Minister may exempt for a
  specified period any vessel, or class of vessels, that is en route
  through Canadian waters but is not en route to or departing from a
  port in Canada, from the application of any provision of Part 3
  (Personnel), 4 (Safety) or 9 (Pollution Prevention --- Department of
  Transport) if the Minister is of the opinion that the provision is
  substantially similar to a provision of the laws of another state to
  which the vessel or class of vessels is subject.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication --- [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Notice of every exemption granted under subsection
  (2), (2.1) or (3) must be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport or the Minister of
  Fisheries and Oceans may authorize any person employed in the federal
  public administration or any police officer, police constable,
  constable or other person employed for the preservation and
  maintenance of the public peace, or any class of any of those persons,
  that that Minister considers proper to exercise any of the powers and
  perform any of the duties and functions that may be or are required to
  be exercised or performed by that Minister under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 10
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
- 2018, c. 27, s. 689
- [2023, c. 26, s. 352](#1410264-1413659){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10-20181213.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order --- Minister of Transport

- **[[10.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may make
  an interim order that contains any provision that may be contained in
  a regulation made, under this Act, on the recommendation of only that
  Minister, if he or she believes that immediate action is required to
  deal with a direct or indirect risk to marine safety or to the marine
  environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization to make interim order

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may, subject to any
  restrictions or conditions that the Minister may specify, authorize
  the Deputy Minister of Transport to make, for the purpose referred to
  in subsection (1), an interim order that contains any provision that
  may be contained in a regulation made, under this Act, on the
  recommendation of only that Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interim order made under this section has effect
  from the time that it is made or from any later date that may be
  specified in the interim order, but ceases to have effect on the
  earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which it is repealed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a regulation made under this Act
    that has the same effect as the interim order comes into force;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} one year after the effective date of the interim
    order or any shorter period that may be specified in the interim
    order, unless the effective period is extended by the Minister of
    Transport or the Governor in Council;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the day that is specified in the order of the
    Minister of Transport extending the interim order, if the Minister
    extends the effective period of the interim order, unless the
    effective period is extended by the Governor in Council; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the day that is specified in the order of the
    Governor in Council, if the Governor in Council extends the
    effective period of the interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension --- Minister of Transport

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If the period specified in the interim order is
  less than one year, the Minister of Transport may extend the effective
  period of the interim order for a period that ends no more than one
  year after the effective date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension --- Governor in Council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may extend the effective
  period of the interim order for a period of no more than two years
  after the end of the applicable period referred to in paragraph (2)(c)
  or after the day referred to in paragraph (2)(c.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with interim order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every person or vessel subject to an interim order
  shall comply with it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of unpublished order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person or vessel may be found guilty of an offence
  consisting of a contravention of an interim order that, at the time of
  the alleged contravention, had not been published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) unless it is proved that, at the
  time of the alleged contravention, the person or vessel had been
  notified of the interim order or reasonable steps had been taken to
  bring the purport of the interim order to the notice of those persons
  or vessels likely to be affected by it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does
  not apply to an interim order or an order extending the interim order.
  However, any such order must be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) within 23 days after it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling of order

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport shall ensure that a copy of
  each interim order is tabled in each House of Parliament within 15
  days after it is made. The copy is to be sent to the Clerk of the
  House if the House is not sitting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 27, s. 690
- [2023, c. 26, s. 353](#1410269-1413671){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410269-1413671.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-10.1-20181213.html "Link to previous version of section 10.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inspections by Marine Safety Inspectors and Others]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50869 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of marine safety inspectors

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Marine
  safety inspectors are appointed or deployed under the [Public Service
  Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorizations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may authorize a marine
  safety inspector to exercise any power or perform any duty or function
  of the Minister under this Act, including quasi-judicial powers and
  the administration of examinations referred to in subsection 16(2), or
  to carry out inspections under section 211, including the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} inspections of hulls;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inspections of machinery;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} inspections of equipment;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} inspections respecting pollution prevention for
    the purpose of Part 8 (Pollution Prevention and Response ---
    Department of Transport and Department of Fisheries and Oceans);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} inspections respecting the protection of the marine
    environment for the purpose of Part 9 (Pollution Prevention ---
    Department of Transport); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} inspections of cargo.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport must furnish every marine
  safety inspector with a certificate of designation authorizing the
  inspector to carry out inspections under section 211 or to exercise
  any power or perform any duty or function of the Minister under this
  Act, including any quasi-judicial powers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties and powers

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A marine safety inspector may exercise only those
  powers and perform only those duties and functions that are referred
  to in the inspector's certificate of designation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Marine safety inspectors are not personally liable
  for anything they do or omit to do in good faith under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 11
- 2014, c. 29, s. 59
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorizing others to inspect

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister of Transport may authorize any person, class of persons,
  classification society or other organization to issue any Canadian
  maritime document under this Act or to carry out inspections under
  section 211 if the Minister determines that the person, class of
  persons, classification society or other organization is qualified to
  issue the document or carry out the inspection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of authorization

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport must furnish the person,
  classification society or other organization with a certificate of
  authorization specifying the documents they are authorized to issue,
  the inspections they are authorized to carry out and any limitations
  on the powers they may exercise under subsection 211(4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection records

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person, classification society or other
  organization authorized to carry out inspections must keep a record of
  each inspection in the form and manner specified by the Minister of
  Transport and, on request, provide the record to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person, classification society or other
  organization that does not, in respect of anything that they were
  authorized to inspect, issue a certificate because the requirements of
  the regulations have not been met must deliver their report in respect
  of that inspection to a marine safety inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The person, classification society or other
  organization is not personally liable for anything they do or omit to
  do in good faith under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 12
- [2023, c. 26, s. 354](#1410280-1413675){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410280-1413675.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** The Minister of Transport
may authorize any person or class of persons to audit inspections
carried out under section 211. The auditor may exercise the powers under
that section of the person, classification society or other organization
whose inspections are being audited.

### [Authorized Representative]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50902 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized representative

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  Canadian vessel must have a person --- the authorized representative
  --- who is responsible under this Act for acting with respect to all
  matters relating to the vessel that are not otherwise assigned by this
  Act to any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualified person, bare-boat charterer
  or owner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), the authorized
  representative of a Canadian vessel is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel whose owner has entered
    into an arrangement with a qualified person, including an operator
    of the vessel, under which they are responsible for the matters
    referred to in subsection (1), the qualified person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel described in section 48,
    the bare-boat charterer; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the owner of the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative if more than one owner

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a Canadian vessel that is owned by
  more than one person, the owners must appoint as the authorized
  representative one of themselves or, in accordance with paragraph
  (2)(a), a qualified person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative of foreign corporation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a Canadian vessel that is owned by a
  corporation incorporated under the laws of a state other than Canada,
  the authorized representative must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a subsidiary of the corporation incorporated under
    the laws of Canada or a province;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an employee or a director in Canada of a branch
    office of the corporation that is carrying on business in Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a ship management company incorporated under the
    laws of Canada or a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acts or omissions of authorized
  representative binding

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The owner of a Canadian vessel is bound by the acts
  or omissions of their authorized representative with respect to the
  matters referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 14
- [2023, c. 26, s. 355](#1410282-1413685){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410282-1413685.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 14"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 29,
s. 72\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Canadian Maritime Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-50918 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application for a Canadian maritime document must be made in the form
  and manner, include the information and be accompanied by the
  documents specified by the Minister of Transport.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the specified information or
  documents, the Minister of Transport may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require that an applicant provide evidence,
    including declarations, that the Minister considers necessary to
    establish that the requirements for the issuance of the document
    have been met;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the Canadian maritime document is in respect of
    a person,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} set an examination that the person must undergo,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} administer the examination; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the document is in respect of a vessel, require
    that the vessel or its machinery or equipment undergo any
    inspections that the Minister considers necessary to establish that
    the requirements for the issuance of the document have been met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cheating

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall cheat on an examination referred to
  in paragraph (2)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to issue

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may refuse to issue a
  Canadian maritime document if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has not met the requirements for the
    issuance of the document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has acted fraudulently or improperly
    or has misrepresented a material fact;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion that the public
    interest and, in particular, the record of the applicant or of a
    principal of the applicant warrant it;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has not paid a fee for services
    related to the document or has not paid a fine or penalty imposed on
    them under this Act; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian maritime document applied
    for under Part 3 (Personnel) by a master or crew member,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the master or crew member was on board a vessel
      that contravened any of sections 5.3 to 5.5 of the [Coastal
      Fisheries Protection Act](/eng/acts/C-33) and knew, when the
      contravention occurred, that the vessel was committing the
      contravention, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the master or crew member has been found guilty
      of an offence related to their duties on a vessel or has committed
      a violation in respect of which a notice of violation was issued
      under paragraph 229(1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice after refusal to issue

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport must, immediately after
  refusing to issue a Canadian maritime document, give the applicant a
  notice

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confirming the refusal and providing all relevant
    information concerning the grounds on which the Minister has refused
    to issue the document; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} indicating, in the case of a refusal to issue a
    document under Part 3 (Personnel) on the grounds set out in
    paragraph (4)(a), (b), (c) or (e), the address at which, and the
    date, being thirty days after the notice is given, on or before
    which, the applicant may file a request for a review of the
    Minister's decision.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 29, s. 72\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 16, c. 29, s. 72
- 2012, c. 31, s. 156
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20030630.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

- **[[16.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant who receives a notice under subsection 16(5) may, on or
  before the date specified in the notice or within any further time
  that the Tribunal on application allows, file a written request for a
  review of the decision if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the request is in respect of a Canadian maritime
    document that is issued under Part 3 (Personnel); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the grounds for refusing to issue the document are
    set out in paragraph 16(4)(a), (b), (c) or (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and place for review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a request filed under subsection (1),
  the Tribunal must appoint a time and place for the review and must
  notify the Minister of Transport and the applicant of the time and
  place in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review procedure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The member of the Tribunal assigned to conduct the
  review must provide the Minister of Transport and the applicant with
  an opportunity consistent with procedural fairness and natural justice
  to present evidence and make representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person not compelled to testify

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In a review of a decision made under subparagraph
  16(4)(e)(ii) on the ground that an applicant has committed a violation
  in respect of which a notice of violation has been issued to the
  applicant, the applicant is not required, and must not be compelled,
  to give any evidence or testimony in the matter before the matter that
  gave rise to the issue of the notice has been disposed of in
  accordance with sections 232 to 232.2.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The member may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a decision made under paragraph
    16(4)(e), confirm the decision or substitute his or her own
    determination; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, confirm the decision or refer
    the matter back to the Minister of Transport for reconsideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of validity

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  Canadian maritime document is valid for the period specified by the
  Minister of Transport. The Minister may, on application made before a
  document expires and in the form and manner specified by the Minister,
  extend the period if the Minister is satisfied that it is not feasible
  to issue a new document before the document expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by this Act or the
  regulations, no person, other than the person to whom it was issued or
  their representative, shall possess a Canadian maritime document
  issued under Part 3 (Personnel).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of document

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** Every holder of a
Canadian maritime document shall produce it to the Minister of Transport
on demand.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lost documents

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** If a Canadian maritime
document is mislaid, lost or destroyed, the Minister of Transport may
issue a replacement document on application made by the holder of the
document in the form and manner and including the information and
accompanied by the documents specified by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension, cancellation and refusal to
renew

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 20.1, the
  Minister of Transport may suspend, cancel or refuse to renew a
  Canadian maritime document if the Minister is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements for the issuance of the document
    are no longer met;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a term or condition attached to the document has
    been contravened;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the document was obtained by any fraudulent or
    improper means or a misrepresentation of any material fact;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the holder of the document has not paid a fine or
    penalty imposed on them under this Act;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the holder of the document has contravened a
    provision of this Act or the regulations that the Minister is
    responsible for administering;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian maritime document issued
    under Part 3 (Personnel) to a master or crew member,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the master or crew member is incompetent or has
      committed an act of misconduct,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the master or crew member was on board a vessel
      that contravened any of sections 5.3 to 5.5 of the [Coastal
      Fisheries Protection Act](/eng/acts/C-33) and knew, when the
      contravention occurred, that the vessel was committing the
      contravention, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the master or crew member has been found guilty
      of an offence related to their duties on a vessel; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a refusal to renew,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has not paid a fee for services
      related to the document, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion that the public
      interest and, in particular, the record of the applicant or of a
      principal of the applicant warrant it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of document

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a Canadian maritime document is suspended or
  cancelled, the person who held it shall return it on demand to the
  Minister of Transport.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 20, c. 29, s. 72
- 2012, c. 31, s. 157
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-20-20030630.html "Link to previous version of section 20"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice before suspension or cancellation

**[[20.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20.1 .sectionLabel}** The Minister of
Transport must, before suspending or cancelling a Canadian maritime
document issued under Part 3 (Personnel), give the holder 30 days notice
of the proposed suspension or cancellation. The notice must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide the holder with all relevant information
  concerning the grounds on which the Minister proposes to suspend or
  cancel the document; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} indicate, in the case of a proposed suspension or
  cancellation of a document on the grounds referred to in paragraph
  20(1)(a), (b), (c), (e) or (f) or subparagraph 20(1)(g)(ii), the
  address at which, and the date, being 30 days after the notice is
  given, on or before which, the applicant may file a request for a
  review of the Minister's decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

- **[[20.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may
  suspend or cancel a Canadian maritime document issued under Part 3
  (Personnel) without complying with section 20.1 if, on [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application by the Minister, the Tribunal
  determines that compliance with that section is not in the interest of
  public safety.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision within 24 hours

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application by the Minister of Transport under
  subsection (1) must be heard by a member of the Tribunal, sitting
  alone, whose determination shall be made within 24 hours after the
  application is filed with the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may, within 24 hours after
  the determination, appeal the determination to the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision within 48 hours

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The appeal panel of the Tribunal assigned to hear the
  appeal shall make a decision within 48 hours after the appeal is filed
  with the Tribunal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice after suspension, cancellation or
refusal to renew

**[[20.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20.3 .sectionLabel}** Except where notice
of a proposed suspension or cancellation of a Canadian maritime document
is given under section 20.1, the Minister of Transport must, immediately
after suspending, cancelling or refusing to renew a Canadian maritime
document, give the holder a notice that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} confirms the suspension, cancellation or refusal and
  provides all relevant information concerning the grounds on which the
  Minister suspended, cancelled or refused to renew the document; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} indicates, in the case of the suspension or
  cancellation of, or the refusal to renew, a document on the grounds
  referred to in paragraph 20(1)(a), (b), (c), (e) or (f) or
  subparagraph 20(1)(g)(ii), the address at which, and the date, being
  30 days after the notice is given, on or before which, the applicant
  may file a request for a review of the Minister's decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

- **[[20.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  holder of a Canadian maritime document that is referred to in a notice
  under section 20.1 or 20.3 may, within the time specified in the
  notice or any further time that the Tribunal on application allows,
  file a written request for a review of the decision referred to in the
  notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A request for a review must not be filed with, or
  accepted by, the Tribunal if the grounds for suspending, cancelling or
  refusing to renew the document are set out in paragraph 20(1)(d) or
  subparagraph 20(1)(g)(i).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of request

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The filing of a request for a review in respect of a
  notice under section 20.1 operates as a stay of the proposed
  suspension or cancellation until the matter is finally disposed of in
  accordance with this section or section 20.5.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and place for review

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a request filed under subsection (1),
  the Tribunal must appoint a time and place for the review and must
  notify the Minister of Transport and the holder of the time and place
  in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review procedure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The member of the Tribunal assigned to conduct the
  review must provide the Minister of Transport and the holder with an
  opportunity consistent with procedural fairness and natural justice to
  present evidence and make representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person not compelled to testify

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In a review of a decision made under paragraph
  20(1)(e), the holder is not required, and must not be compelled, to
  give any evidence or testimony in the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The member may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a decision made under paragraph
    20(1)(e) or subparagraph 20(1)(f)(ii) or (iii), confirm the Minister
    of Transport's decision or substitute his or her own determination;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, confirm the Minister of
    Transport's decision or refer the matter back to the Minister for
    reconsideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal

- **[[20.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The applicant for, or holder of, a
  Canadian maritime document may appeal to the Tribunal a determination
  made under subsection 16.1(5) or 20.4(7), and the Minister of
  Transport may appeal to the Tribunal a determination made under
  paragraph 16.1(5)(a) or 20.4(7)(a). The time limit for making an
  appeal is 30 days after the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of right of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party that does not appear at a review hearing is
  not entitled to appeal a determination, unless they establish that
  there was sufficient reason to justify their absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The appeal panel of the Tribunal assigned to hear the
  appeal may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination made under paragraph
    16.1(5)(a) or 20.4(7)(a), dismiss the appeal, or allow the appeal
    and substitute its own decision; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination made under paragraph
    16.1(5)(b) or 20.4(7)(b), dismiss the appeal or refer the matter
    back to the Minister of Transport for reconsideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

### [Other Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51045 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of documents to foreign vessels

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The Minister of Transport
may, at the request of the government of a state to which an
international convention, protocol or resolution listed in Schedule 1
applies, issue in respect of a vessel registered in that state any
document provided for by the convention, protocol or resolution, other
than a Canadian maritime document, if the Minister is satisfied, in the
same manner as in the case of a Canadian vessel, that the document may
properly be issued. A document issued under this section must contain a
statement that it has been issued at the request of that government.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 21
- [2023, c. 26, s. 356](#1410284-1413691){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410284-1413691.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign documents

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The Minister of Transport
may refuse to accept a foreign document required for the operation of a
foreign vessel if in the Minister's opinion the conditions under which
the document was issued do not comply with international agreements to
which Canada is a signatory.

### [General Prohibitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51050 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of documents, fraud,
obstruction and movement of detained vessel

**[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** No person shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} wilfully destroy a document that is required under
  this Act to be kept;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} make or cause to be made a false entry in a log book
  required under this Act to be kept with intent to mislead or wilfully
  omit to make an entry;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} wilfully obstruct any inspector or other person or
  classification society or other organization that is exercising powers
  or performing duties under this Act;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} knowingly provide false or misleading information or
  make a false or misleading statement, either orally or in writing, to
  a person, classification society or other organization that is
  exercising powers or performing duties under this Act; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} except as authorized under this Act, wilfully move a
  vessel that has been detained under this Act.

### [Tonnage Measurers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51058 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of tonnage measurers

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** The Minister of Transport
may appoint persons, to be known as tonnage measurers, to calculate
vessels' tonnage under this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees and travel expenses

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** A tonnage measurer may
withhold the tonnage certificate in respect of a vessel until the person
requesting it pays the tonnage measurer's fees and travel expenses. The
Minister of Transport may set limits on the fees and travel expenses
that may be charged.

### [Marine Technical Review Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51063 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of ensuring the safety of the marine industry, the Marine
  Technical Review Board is established to make decisions on
  applications for an exemption from, or the replacement of, any
  requirement under the regulations or an interim order made under
  subsection 10.1(1) or (1.1) in respect of a Canadian vessel or in
  respect of the issuance of a Canadian maritime document to a person,
  other than a requirement with respect to fees, charges, costs or
  expenses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of the Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board consists of a Chair, a National Vice-Chair
  and a maximum of five Regional Vice-Chairs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chair

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chair is appointed by the Minister of Transport
  and must be an employee of the Department of Transport who has
  expertise in marine matters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vice-Chairs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Vice-Chairs are appointed by the Chair and must
  be employees of the Department of Transport who have expertise in
  marine matters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chair may delegate the Chair's powers and duties
  to the National Vice-Chair.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of the
  Chair or if the office of Chair is vacant, the National Vice-Chair may
  exercise all the powers and perform all the duties of the Chair.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 26
- [2023, c. 26, s. 357](#1410286-1413698){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410286-1413698.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Striking panels

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  an application is made under subsection 28(1), the Chair must strike a
  panel of at least three persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constitution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each panel consists of the Chair and the persons
  appointed by the Chair.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expertise

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any person to be appointed to a panel, other than a
  Vice-Chair, must have expertise in the matter in respect of which the
  panel is struck.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fee and expenses

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each panellist may be paid

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount fixed by the Minister of Transport for
    each day or part of a day that the panellist is performing duties
    under section 28, unless they are employed in the federal public
    administration; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable travel and living expenses incurred by
    the panellist in the course of their duties under section 28.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Casting vote

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Matters before a panel are decided by a majority of
  the members of the panel. The Chair has a second vote in the case of a
  tie.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Panel decisions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a decision of a panel is a
  decision of the Marine Technical Review Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 27
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-27-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person may, in respect of a requirement under the regulations or an
  interim order made under subsection 10.1(1) or (1.1) that applies in
  respect of a Canadian vessel or in respect of the issuance of a
  Canadian maritime document to a person, apply to the Marine Technical
  Review Board for a decision to exempt the applicant from the
  requirement or to replace it with another requirement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and manner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made in the form and manner,
  include the information and be accompanied by the documents specified
  by the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the specified information and
  documents, the Board may require an applicant to provide evidence,
  including declarations, that the Board considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grant of application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the panel struck to decide the application is
  satisfied that the exemption or replacement is in the public interest
  and would not jeopardize marine safety or the marine environment and,
  in the case of an application to replace a requirement with respect to
  safety, the replacement would result in an equivalent or greater level
  of safety, the panel is to issue a decision granting the application,
  subject to any conditions and for the period that the panel considers
  appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant to be notified

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chair is to notify the applicant of the decision
  to grant or deny the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the decision is to grant the application, the
  Chair must publish the decision in the manner that the Chair considers
  appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to inform

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a person has reason to believe that a decision to
  grant an application was based on false or misleading information or
  that the information provided with the application has changed, the
  person shall inform the Chair without delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions based on false or misleading
  information

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the Chair has reason to believe that a decision to
  grant an application was based on false or misleading information or
  that the information provided with the application has changed, the
  Chair may strike a panel in accordance with section 27 and the panel
  may confirm, cancel or vary the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A contravention of a requirement that is substituted
  for another requirement as a result of a panel decision under
  subsection (4) is deemed to be a contravention of the original
  requirement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Board must make rules respecting its procedure,
  with the approval of the Minister of Transport.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after the end of each fiscal
  year, the Chair must submit to the Minister of Transport a report of
  the Board's operations in that year.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 28
- [2023, c. 26, s. 358](#1410288-1413705){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410288-1413705.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-28-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 28"){.PITLink}
:::

### [International Conventions, Protocols and Resolutions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51117 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Schedule 1

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Schedule 1 lists the international
  conventions, protocols and resolutions that Canada has signed that
  relate to matters that are within the scope of this Act and that the
  Minister of Transport has determined should be brought into force, in
  whole or in part, in Canada by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Schedule 2

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Schedule 2 lists the international conventions,
  protocols and resolutions that Canada has signed that relate to
  matters that are within the scope of this Act and that the Minister of
  Fisheries and Oceans has determined should be brought into force, in
  whole or in part, in Canada by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Codes and guidelines

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A convention or protocol includes any code or
  guideline that is attached to it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additions to Schedule 1 or 2

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, by order, add international conventions,
  protocols and resolutions described in subsection 29(1) to Schedule 1
  or described in subsection 29(2) to Schedule 2.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be tabled and referred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport is to cause a copy of each
  order related to Schedule 1 and the Minister of Fisheries and Oceans
  is to cause a copy of each order related to Schedule 2, together with
  a description of the objectives of the convention, protocol or
  resolution, to be laid before each House of Parliament on any of the
  first 10 days on which that House is sitting after the order is made.
  The order stands referred to the appropriate standing committee of
  each House.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deletions from Schedule 1 or 2

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, by order, delete an international convention, protocol or
resolution from Schedule 1 or 2 or amend Schedule 1 or 2 if the
amendment would not, in the opinion of the Governor in Council, result
in a material substantive change.

### [Incorporation by Reference]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51132 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Externally produced material

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  regulation made under this Act may incorporate by reference material
  produced by a person or body other than the Minister who recommended
  to the Governor in Council that the regulation be made, including by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an organization established for the purpose of
    writing standards, including an organization accredited by the
    Standards Council of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an industrial or a trade organization; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a government, a government agency or an
    international body.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reproduced or translated material

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under this Act may incorporate by
  reference material that the Minister who recommended to the Governor
  in Council that the regulation be made reproduces or translates from
  material produced by a person or body other than that Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with any adaptations of form and reference that
    will facilitate its incorporation into the regulation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a form that sets out only the parts of the
    material that apply for the purposes of the regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jointly produced material

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under this Act may incorporate by
  reference material that the Minister who recommended to the Governor
  in Council that the regulation be made produces jointly with another
  government or government agency for the purpose of harmonizing the
  regulation with other laws.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Internally produced standards

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under this Act may incorporate by
  reference technical or explanatory material that the Minister who
  recommended to the Governor in Council that the regulation be made
  produces, such as

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifications, classifications, illustrations,
    graphs and other information of a technical nature; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} test methods, procedures, operational standards,
    safety standards and performance standards of a technical nature.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Material produced by Minister of
  Transport

  [(4.01)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under paragraph 35.1(1)(k),
  120(1)(f) or (k), 136(1)(a), (f) or (g) or 190(1)(a) may incorporate
  by reference material that the Minister of Transport produces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variations of externally produced
  material

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under this Act on the
  recommendation of the Minister of Transport or on the joint
  recommendation of that Minister and the Minister of Natural Resources
  may incorporate by reference material produced by the Minister of
  Transport that varies material incorporated by reference under
  subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of incorporation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Material referred to in subsections (1) to (4.01) may
  be incorporated by reference as amended from time to time or as it
  exists on a particular date. Material referred to in subsection (4.1)
  that is to be incorporated by reference must be incorporated as it
  exists on a particular date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporated material is not a
  regulation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Material that is incorporated by reference in a
  regulation made under this Act is not a regulation for the purposes of
  the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) to (6) are for greater certainty and
  do not limit any authority to make regulations incorporating material
  by reference that exists apart from those subsections.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 32
- 2012, c. 31, s. 158
- [2023, c. 26, s. 359](#1410290-1413721){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410290-1413721.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20121214.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty, no
person or vessel may be convicted of an offence or subjected to a
penalty for the contravention of a provision of a regulation made under
this Act that incorporates material by reference unless it is proved
that, at the time of the alleged contravention,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the material was reasonably accessible to the person
  or vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable steps had been taken to ensure that the
  material was accessible to persons or vessels likely to be affected by
  the regulation; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the material had been published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

### [Orders and Directions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51164 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In writing

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  this Act requires or authorizes an order or a direction to be given or
  made by the Minister of Transport to a person who is not an official
  of the Department of Transport, the order or direction must be given
  or made in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order or direction referred to in subsection (1)
  is not a [[statutory instrument]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in
  the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 34
- 2018, c. 27, s. 691
:::

[Previous
Version](section-34-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51172 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- Minister of Transport

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister of
  Transport, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 29, s. 72\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the issuance, amendment, suspension,
    reinstatement, cancellation or renewal of Canadian maritime
    documents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 29, s. 72\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} implementing, in whole or in part, an international
    convention, protocol or resolution that is listed in Schedule 1, as
    amended from time to time, including regulations

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} implementing it in respect of persons, vessels,
      oil handling facilities or hazardous and noxious substances
      handling facilities to which it does not apply,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} establishing stricter standards than it sets
      out, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} establishing additional or complementary
      standards to those it sets out if the Governor in Council is
      satisfied that the additional or complementary standards meet the
      objectives of the convention, protocol or resolution;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting record keeping, information management
    and reporting for the purposes of this Part or Part 2 (Registration,
    Listing and Recording), 3 (Personnel), 4 (Safety), 6 (Incidents,
    Accidents and Casualties), 8 (Pollution Prevention and Response ---
    Department of Transport and Department of Fisheries and Oceans) to
    the extent that the Minister of Transport is responsible for that
    Part, 9 (Pollution Prevention --- Department of Transport), 10
    (Pleasure Craft) or 11 (Enforcement --- Department of Transport) or
    the regulations made under subsection 136(1);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the form and manner of giving notice
    under this Part or Part 2 (Registration, Listing and Recording), 3
    (Personnel), 4 (Safety), 8 (Pollution Prevention and Response ---
    Department of Transport and Department of Fisheries and Oceans) to
    the extent that the Minister of Transport is responsible for that
    Part, 9 (Pollution Prevention --- Department of Transport), 10
    (Pleasure Craft) or 11 (Enforcement --- Department of Transport) or
    the regulations made under subsection 136(1);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting fees, charges, costs and expenses to be
    paid in relation to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the administration of this Part or Part 2
      (Registration, Listing and Recording), 3 (Personnel), 4 (Safety),
      6 (Incidents, Accidents and Casualties), 8 (Pollution Prevention
      and Response --- Department of Transport and Department of
      Fisheries and Oceans) to the extent that the Minister of Transport
      is responsible for that Part, 9 (Pollution Prevention ---
      Department of Transport), 10 (Pleasure Craft), 11 (Enforcement ---
      Department of Transport) or 12 (Miscellaneous) to the extent that
      the Minister of Transport is responsible for that Part or the
      regulations made under any of those Parts or under subsection
      136(1), including the development of those regulations, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of any of those Parts or
      regulations;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} specifying the services for the purposes of
    paragraph 36.1(1)(d); and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} specifying the services to which section 36.1 does
    not apply or the circumstances in which that section does not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aircraft

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, regulations made under
  paragraph (1)(d) in respect of preventing collisions may apply to
  aircraft on or over Canadian waters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- Minister of Fisheries
  and Oceans

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister of Fisheries and Oceans, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} implementing, in whole or in part, an international
    convention, protocol or resolution that is listed in Schedule 2, as
    amended from time to time, including regulations

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} implementing it in respect of persons or vessels
      to which it does not apply,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} establishing stricter standards than it sets
      out, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} establishing additional or complementary
      standards to those it sets out if the Governor in Council is
      satisfied that the additional or complementary standards meet the
      objectives of the convention, protocol or resolution;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting record keeping, information management
    and reporting for the purposes of Part 5 (Navigation Services) or 8
    (Pollution Prevention and Response --- Department of Transport and
    Department of Fisheries and Oceans), to the extent that the Minister
    of Fisheries and Oceans is responsible for those Parts;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the form and manner of giving notice
    under Part 5 (Navigation Services) or 8 (Pollution Prevention and
    Response --- Department of Transport and Department of Fisheries and
    Oceans), to the extent that the Minister of Fisheries and Oceans is
    responsible for those Parts; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the setting and payment of fees for
    services provided in the administration of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Part 5 (Navigation Services) or 8 (Pollution
      Prevention and Response --- Department of Transport and Department
      of Fisheries and Oceans), to the extent that the Minister of
      Fisheries and Oceans is responsible for those Parts, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} regulations made under subsection 136(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- protection of marine
environment

- **[[35.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the
  recommendation of the Minister of Transport, make regulations
  respecting the protection of the marine environment from the impacts
  of navigation and shipping activities, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the design, construction, manufacture
    and maintenance of vessels or classes of vessels;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specifying the machinery, equipment and supplies
    that are required or prohibited on board vessels or classes of
    vessels;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the design, construction, manufacture,
    maintenance, storage, testing, approval, arrangement and use of the
    machinery, equipment and supplies of vessels or classes of vessels;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the requirements that vessels, or
    classes of vessels, and their machinery and equipment are to meet;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} requiring the obtaining of certificates certifying
    that any of the requirements referred to in paragraph (d) are met;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} specifying the terms and conditions of certificates
    referred to in paragraph (e);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the inspections and testing of vessels,
    or classes of vessels, and their machinery, equipment and supplies;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} respecting procedures and practices that are to be
    followed;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} respecting the development, maintenance and
    implementation of a management system that sets out the manner in
    which marine environment protection measures are to be integrated
    into day-to-day navigation and shipping operations and the criteria
    to which that management system is to conform as well as the
    components that are to be included in the system;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} respecting compulsory routes and recommended
    routes;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} regulating or prohibiting the operation,
    navigation, anchoring, mooring or berthing of vessels or classes of
    vessels; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} regulating or prohibiting the loading or unloading
    of a vessel or a class of vessels.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment by Minister of Transport

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under any of paragraphs (1)(h), (j)
  and (k) may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorize the Minister of Transport to amend, by
    order, that regulation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out the terms and conditions under which the
    regulation may be amended by that order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of effect of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order referred to in subsection (2) has effect
  from the time that it is made and ceases to have effect on the
  earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which it is repealed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one year after the order is made or any shorter
    period that may be specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does
  not apply to an order referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport shall publish, on the
  Department of Transport's Internet site or by any other means that the
  Minister of Transport considers appropriate, a notice of every order
  referred to in subsection (2), as soon as feasible after it is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 27, s. 692
:::

### [Fees, Charges, Costs and Expenses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-51232 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt due to His Majesty

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  fees, charges, costs and expenses imposed under paragraph 35(1)(g) or
  (3)(d) and interest payable on them constitute a debt due to His
  Majesty in right of Canada and may be recovered in any court of
  competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a fee, charge, cost or expense is imposed under
  paragraph 35(1)(g) or (3)(d)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a pleasure craft that is not a
    Canadian vessel, its owner is liable for payment of the fee, charge,
    cost or expense and any interest payable on it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a Canadian vessel, the authorized
    representative, the owner and the master are jointly and severally,
    or solidarily, liable for payment of the fee, charge, cost or
    expense and any interest payable on it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a foreign vessel, its owner and the
    authorized representative are jointly and severally, or solidarily,
    liable for payment of the fee, charge, cost or expense and any
    interest payable on it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the amount of a fee, charge, cost or expense, or
  of interest due on it, owed by an authorized representative or owner
  of a Canadian vessel or by the owner of a foreign vessel, has not been
  paid, the Minister who recommended making the regulation under
  paragraph 35(1)(g) or (3)(d) may, in addition to any other remedy
  available for the collection of the amount and whether or not a
  judgment for the collection of the amount has been obtained, apply to
  the Federal Court for an order authorizing that Minister to seize,
  detain and sell any vessel belonging to the authorized representative
  or the owner, as the case may be. The Court may make the order on the
  terms that the Court considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release on security

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A Minister who obtains an order under subsection (3)
  must release a seized vessel from detention if security in a form
  satisfactory to that Minister for the amount in respect of which the
  vessel was seized is deposited with the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 36
- 2012, c. 31, s. 160(F)
- [2023, c. 26, s. 362](#1410302-1413740){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410302-1413740.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36-20121214.html "Link to previous version of section 36"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement --- cost recovery

- **[[36.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may enter
  into an agreement with any person or organization respecting any
  matter for which a regulation made under paragraph 35(1)(g) could
  prescribe a fee, charge, cost or expense.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If both an agreement entered into under
  subsection (1) and a regulation made under paragraph 35(1)(g) relate
  to the same matter, the regulation does not apply to the person or
  organization that has entered into the agreement in respect of the
  matter for which payment is required under the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When the Minister of Transport enters into an
  undertaking in respect of a matter for which payment of an amount is
  required from the other party under the terms of an agreement entered
  into under subsection (1), the undertaking is deemed, for the purposes
  of recovering that amount, not to be a duty of the Minister under this
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt due to Her Majesty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All amounts payable under an agreement entered into
  under subsection (1) and any interest payable on them constitute a
  debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and may be recovered as
  such in any court of competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Spending

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Transport may spend the amounts
  received under an agreement entered into under subsection (1) in the
  fiscal year in which they are received or in the next fiscal year.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 316
- [2023, c. 26, s. 363](#1410304-1413742){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410304-1413742.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36.01-20170622.html "Link to previous version of section 36.01"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services provided by third parties

- **[[36.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise provided in the
  regulations, any person or organization that provides, in the exercise
  of powers or the performance of duties under this Act in accordance
  with an agreement or arrangement entered into by the Minister of
  Transport under paragraph 10(1)(c), any of the following services may,
  if that person or organization is not part of the federal public
