  subsection (1) is liable on summary conviction to a fine of not more
  than \$100,000.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 192
- [2023, c. 26, s. 405](#1410450-1413949){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410450-1413949.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-192-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 192"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court orders

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** If an offender is
convicted of an offence under this Part, in addition to imposing any
other punishment that may be imposed under this Act, the court may,
having regard to the nature of the offence and the circumstances
surrounding its commission, make an order having any or all of the
following effects:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the offender from performing any act or
  engaging in any activity that may result in the continuation or
  repetition of the offence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to publish the facts relating
  to the conviction;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} directing the offender to submit to the Minister, on
  application by the Minister made within three years after the date of
  the conviction, any information with respect to the offender's
  activities that the court considers appropriate and just in the
  circumstances;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a discharge, directing the offender to
  pay an amount for the purpose of conducting research into the
  ecological use and disposal of the pollutant in respect of which the
  offence was committed; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} requiring the offender to comply with any other
  reasonable conditions that the court considers appropriate and just in
  the circumstances for securing the offender's good conduct and
  preventing the offender from repeating the same offence or committing
  other offences.

## [PART 10]{.HLabel1}[Pleasure Craft]{.HTitleText1} {#h-52842 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52843 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[enforcement officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[enforcement officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a member of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a member of any harbour or river police force;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a member of any provincial, county or municipal
      police force; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any person, or member of a class of persons,
      designated under subsection 196(1). ([agent de
      l'autorité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pleasure craft safety
    inspector designated under subsection
    195(1). ([inspecteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence issued for a
    pleasure craft under this Part. ([permis]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Transport. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pollutant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pollutant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a substance that, if added to any waters, would
      degrade or alter or form part of a process of degradation or
      alteration of the quality of the waters to an extent that is
      detrimental to their use by humans or by an animal or a plant that
      is useful to humans; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any water that contains a substance in such a
      quantity or concentration, or that has been so treated, processed
      or changed, by heat or other means, from a natural state, that it
      would, if added to any waters, degrade or alter or form part of a
      process of degradation or alteration of the quality of the waters
      to an extent that is detrimental to their use by humans or by an
      animal or a plant that is useful to humans.

    It includes oil, hazardous and noxious substances and any substance
    or class of substances that is prescribed for the purpose of Part 8
    (Pollution Prevention and Response --- Department of Transport and
    Department of Fisheries and Oceans) to be a
    pollutant. ([polluant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 194
- 2005, c. 29, s. 31
- [2023, c. 26, s. 406](#1410457-1413954){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410457-1413954.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-194-20051005.html "Link to previous version of section 194"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inspections]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52856 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

- **[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may designate persons or classes of persons as pleasure craft
  safety inspectors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must furnish every inspector with a
  certificate of designation as a pleasure craft safety inspector
  authorizing the inspector to carry out inspections under sections 196
  and 198.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Inspectors are not personally liable for anything
  they do or omit to do in good faith under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may designate persons or classes of persons as enforcement
  officers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections --- general

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer may inspect a pleasure craft
  or any of its machinery or equipment for the purpose of ensuring
  compliance with any provision of this Part, other than section 197, or
  the regulations made under this Part, other than the regulations made
  under paragraph 207(1)(f), (g), (i) or (j).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections --- structural integrity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An inspector may inspect a pleasure craft or any of
  its machinery or equipment for structural integrity or compliance with
  the regulations made under any of paragraphs 207(1)(f) to (m).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Enforcement officers acting under subsection (2) and
  inspectors acting under subsection (3) may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stop or board the craft at any reasonable time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct any person to put into operation or cease
    operating any machinery or equipment on the craft;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} direct that the craft not be moved until the
    inspection is completed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} direct any person to move the craft to a safe place
    if the officer or inspector has reasonable grounds to believe that
    it does not meet the requirements of this Part or the regulations
    made under this Part or exposes any person to serious danger, and
    direct that it not be operated until it meets those requirements or
    no longer exposes any person to serious danger; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} direct any person to move the craft to a safe place
    if the officer or inspector has reasonable grounds to believe that
    the operator does not meet the requirements of the regulations made
    under this Part, and direct that the operator not operate it until
    the operator meets those requirements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to assist

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The owner or person in charge of a pleasure craft and
  every person on board shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give an officer or inspector all reasonable
    assistance to enable them to carry out an inspection and exercise
    any power conferred by this section; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} produce to an officer or inspector any document, or
    provide them with any information, that the officer or inspector may
    reasonably require, for the administration of this Part or Part 5
    (Navigation Services) or the regulations made under either of those
    Parts.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturers and importers

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every manufacturer, builder or
  importer of a pleasure craft shall ensure that it is constructed in
  accordance with the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vendors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall, in the course of a commercial
  enterprise, sell a pleasure craft that does not display a plate or
  label if required to do so by the regulations made under paragraph
  207(1)(h).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

- **[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  inspector may, for the purpose of ensuring that a manufacturer,
  importer or vendor is in compliance with section 197,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter any place, other than a dwelling-house, where
    the inspector has reasonable grounds to believe a pleasure craft is
    located;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} examine anything that the inspector finds and take
    samples of it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} conduct any tests or analyses and take any
    measurements;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} inspect any books, records, electronic data or
    other documents that the inspector believes may contain information
    that is relevant to the inspection;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any computer system in the
    place where the inspection is being carried out to examine any data
    contained in or available to the computer system;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} reproduce or cause to be reproduced any record from
    the data in the form of a print-out or other intelligible output;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} take any document or other thing from the place
    where the inspection is being carried out for examination or, in the
    case of a document, copying; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any copying equipment in
    the place where the inspection is being carried out to make copies
    of any documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to assist

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When an inspector carries out an inspection under
  subsection (1), the importer, manufacturer or vendor of the pleasure
  craft, or the owner or person in control of a place where the pleasure
  craft is located, shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give the inspector all reasonable assistance to
    enable them to carry out the inspection and exercise any power
    conferred by this section; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} produce to the inspector any document, or provide
    them with any information, that the inspector may reasonably require
    for the administration of this Part or the regulations made under
    this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of documents and things

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Documents or other things obtained or taken under
  paragraph (1)(g) must be returned as soon as feasible after they are
  no longer required for the inspection or for any proceedings that may
  result from it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of samples

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An inspector who takes a sample under paragraph
  (1)(b) may dispose of it in any manner that they consider appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operation prohibited

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** If an inspector
considers that a pleasure craft does not meet the requirements of this
Part or the regulations made under this Part, the inspector may direct
any person not to operate it until it meets those requirements.

### [Investigations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52908 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stopping and boarding vessels

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** An enforcement officer
who has reasonable grounds to believe that an offence has been committed
or is about to be committed under this Part by a pleasure craft or any
person on board a vessel may stop and board the craft or vessel and take
any reasonable action to ensure public safety or protect the public
interest.

### [Safe Operation of Pleasure Craft]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52911 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** Every operator of a
pleasure craft shall ensure that it meets the requirements of the
regulations made under this Part.

### [Pleasure Craft Licences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52914 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Licensing of pleasure craft

- **[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, if a
  pleasure craft is required by regulations made under this Part to be
  licensed, the owner of the craft must not operate it, or permit it to
  be operated, unless it is licensed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, when the ownership of a
  pleasure craft referred to in subsection (1) changes, the new owner
  must not operate the craft, or permit it to be operated, until the
  licence for that craft is transferred to the new owner in accordance
  with the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 202
- [2023, c. 26, s. 407](#1410459-1413962){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410459-1413962.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application for a licence or for the transfer of a licence must be
  made in the form and manner, include the information and be
  accompanied by the documents specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may issue or transfer a licence to an
  applicant if the Minister is satisfied that all the requirements under
  subsection (1) have been met.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to issue or transfer licence

**[[203.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.1 .sectionLabel}** Despite any other
provision of this Act, the Minister may refuse to issue --- including by
way of renewal --- or transfer a licence for a pleasure craft or may
refuse to issue a replacement licence if the applicant for, or holder
of, the licence is in default of payment of a required fee, charge, cost
or expense in respect of that pleasure craft under this Act or the
[Wrecked, Abandoned or Hazardous Vessels Act](/eng/acts/W-12.3).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 26, s. 408](#1410461-1413966){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410461-1413966.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Licence number

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** No owner of a pleasure
craft in respect of which a pleasure craft licence has been issued shall
operate it or allow it to be operated unless the licence number is
marked on the craft and maintained in the form and manner specified by
the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defacing, etc., licence number

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** No person shall deface,
alter, conceal or remove the licence number that is marked on a pleasure
craft.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lost documents

**[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** If a pleasure craft
licence is mislaid, lost or destroyed, the Minister, on application made
by the holder of the licence in the form and manner and including the
information and accompanied by the documents specified by the Minister,
may issue a replacement licence.

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52931 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations respecting pleasure craft licensing and the safety of
  pleasure craft or classes of pleasure craft and of persons on board,
  including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the issuance, cancellation or transfer
    of pleasure craft licences;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting qualifications, including medical
    fitness, minimum age, degree of knowledge, skill, training and
    experience, for operators of pleasure craft or classes of pleasure
    craft, and the evidence required to prove compliance with those
    qualifications;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the operation of pleasure craft or
    classes of pleasure craft;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting training courses and examinations for
    pleasure craft operators, including the designation and
    accreditation of persons or organizations that provide them;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the issuance, cancellation or suspension
    of documents that demonstrate compliance with any regulations made
    under paragraph (b) or (d);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the design, construction or manufacture
    of pleasure craft or classes of pleasure craft;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the issuance or cancellation of plates
    or labels in respect of pleasure craft that comply with regulations
    made under paragraph (f);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} requiring that plates or labels referred to in
    paragraph (g) be displayed and specifying the manner in which they
    must be displayed;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the construction, manufacture, sale,
    lease, importation or operation of pleasure craft that do not meet
    the requirements of regulations made under paragraph (f);

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} requiring owners, builders, manufacturers,
    importers or vendors of pleasure craft to modify their pleasure
    craft, at their own expense, in order to comply with regulations
    made under paragraph (f);

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} respecting hull identification or serial numbers
    that identify pleasure craft;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} specifying the machinery and equipment that are
    required or prohibited on pleasure craft or classes of pleasure
    craft;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} respecting the design, construction, manufacture,
    maintenance, storage, testing, approval, arrangement and use of a
    pleasure craft's or a class of pleasure craft's machinery and
    equipment;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} respecting the requirements that pleasure craft, or
    classes of pleasure craft, and their machinery and equipment must
    meet;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} respecting documents that must be kept on board
    pleasure craft or classes of pleasure craft; and

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} respecting the reporting of accidents involving
    pleasure craft.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- pollution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} regulating or prohibiting the discharge of
    pollutants from pleasure craft; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} regulating noise emissions from pleasure craft
    engines.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 207
- 2015, c. 3, s. 24(F)
- [2023, c. 26, s. 409(F)](#1410463-1413967){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410463-1413967.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20150226.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52957 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of Act

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who
  contravenes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subsection 197(1) (ensure a pleasure craft is
    constructed in accordance with the regulations); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsection 197(2) (selling a pleasure craft without
    a plate or label).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who commits an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable on summary conviction to a fine of not more
  than \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than one
  year, or to both.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of Act or regulations

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who
  contravenes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under any of paragraphs 196(4)(b)
    to (e) (to operate or cease operating machinery or equipment, to not
    move a pleasure craft or to move a pleasure craft to a safe place);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsection 196(5) (give reasonable assistance,
    produce documents or provide information);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsection 198(2) (give reasonable assistance,
    produce documents or provide information);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under section 199 (not to operate
    a pleasure craft);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} section 201 (ensure that a pleasure craft meets the
    requirements of the regulations);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} section 202 (obtain a licence for a pleasure
    craft);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} section 204 (operating pleasure craft without
    licence number marked);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} section 205 (defacing, altering, concealing or
    removing licence number); or

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a provision of the regulations made under this
    Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who commits an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable on summary conviction to a fine of not more
  than \$25,000.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 209
- [2023, c. 26, s. 410](#1410465-1413972){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410465-1413972.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 11]{.HLabel1}[Enforcement --- Department of Transport]{.HTitleText1} {#h-52979 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52980 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Transport. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[relevant provision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[relevant provision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a provision of this
    Act or the regulations that the Minister is responsible for
    administering. ([disposition visée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 210
- 2005, c. 29, s. 32
- 2014, c. 29, s. 72
- [2019, c. 1, s. 147](#1114286-1196287){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1114286-1196287.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 26, s. 411](#1410467-1413974){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410467-1413974.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-210-20190730.html "Link to previous version of section 210"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inspections]{.HTitleText2} {#h-52989 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized persons and organizations

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine safety inspector referred to in section 11 or a person,
  classification society or other organization authorized to carry out
  inspections under section 12 may, for the purpose of ensuring
  compliance with a relevant provision, board any vessel or enter any
  premises or other place at any reasonable time and carry out any
  inspection that the inspector, person, classification society or other
  organization considers necessary and that the Minister has authorized
  them to carry out.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Living quarters

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Living quarters may not be entered under
  subsection (1) unless they are entered with the consent of the
  occupant, under the authority of a warrant issued under subsection
  (2.1) or for the purpose of ensuring that a vessel complies with a
  relevant provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to issue warrant

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} On [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application, a
  justice of the peace may issue a warrant authorizing a marine safety
  inspector to enter living quarters, subject to any conditions
  specified in the warrant, if the justice is satisfied by information
  on oath that entry to the living quarters

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is necessary for any purpose related to the
    administration of a relevant provision of Part 8; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been refused or there are reasonable grounds
    for believing that it will be refused.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of force

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} A marine safety inspector executing a warrant must
  not use force unless they are accompanied by a peace officer and the
  use of force is specifically authorized in the warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stopping a vessel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of carrying out an inspection, a
  marine safety inspector may direct the master of a vessel to stop the
  vessel or proceed to the place that the inspector may select, and to
  moor, anchor or remain there for any reasonable period that the
  inspector may specify.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction to authorize vessel

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A marine safety inspector may direct a port
  authority or a person in charge of a port authority or place to
  authorize a vessel in respect of which a direction has been made under
  subsection (3) to proceed to the place selected by the inspector and
  moor, anchor or remain there for any reasonable period that the
  inspector may specify.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspections

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In carrying out an inspection, a marine safety
  inspector or, subject to any limitations set out under subsection
  12(2) in their certificate of authorization, a person, classification
  society or other organization authorized to carry out inspections may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} direct any person to answer reasonable questions,
    provide reasonable assistance or put into operation or cease
    operating any machinery or equipment being inspected;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct the master of a vessel to prohibit or limit
    access to any part of the vessel for as long as specified;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} direct the master of a vessel not to move the
    vessel until the inspection is completed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} direct the master of a vessel to muster the crew or
    to carry out any emergency or safety procedure required by the
    regulations;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} direct the operator of an oil handling facility,
    or a person who proposes to operate an oil handling facility, to
    carry out any emergency or safety procedure that is required by the
    regulations or that is described in an oil pollution prevention plan
    or an oil pollution emergency plan referred to in Part 8;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} direct any person who is at the place where the
    inspection is being carried out to produce for inspection, or for
    the purpose of making copies or taking extracts, any document that
    they are required to have, or that the operator of an oil handling
    facility is required to have on site, under a relevant provision;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} take photographs and make video recordings and
    sketches;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} take or remove for analysis samples of any material
    or substance or any biological, chemical or physical agents or
    substances;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any computer system or data
    processing system at the place where the inspection is being carried
    out to examine any data contained in, or available to, the system;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} reproduce or cause to be reproduced any record from
    the data in the form of a print-out or other intelligible output;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} take any document or other thing from the place
    where the inspection is being carried out for examination or, in the
    case of a document, copying; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any copying equipment in
    the place where the inspection is being carried out to make copies
    of any documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of samples

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person who, or organization that, takes a sample
  under paragraph (4)(g) may dispose of it in any manner that the person
  or organization considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of documents and things

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Documents or other things taken under paragraph
  (4)(j) must be returned as soon as feasible after they are no longer
  required for the purpose for which they were taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 211
- 2014, c. 29, s. 73
- [2023, c. 26, s. 412](#1410469-1413977){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410469-1413977.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20141209.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} During an inspection, a marine
  safety inspector may seize and detain anything

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by means of which or in relation to which the
    inspector believes on reasonable grounds that a relevant provision
    has been contravened; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the inspector believes on reasonable grounds
    will afford evidence in respect of a contravention of a relevant
    provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Storage or removal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An inspector may direct the person in charge of the
  place where something was seized to store it in that place or may
  remove it to any other place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of things or forfeiture

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any thing seized must be returned as soon as feasible
  after it is no longer needed for any proceedings unless it cannot be
  brought into compliance with the relevant provisions and the Minister
  directs that it not be returned, in which case it is forfeited to Her
  Majesty in right of Canada after it is no longer needed for any
  proceedings and may be disposed of at the expense of the person from
  whom it was seized.

### [Clearance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53032 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No departure without clearance

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
regulations, no vessel shall depart from a port in Canada unless
clearance has been granted.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting clearance

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** Any person who is
authorized under an Act of Parliament to grant clearance of a vessel at
a port in Canada is not to grant it unless satisfied that the master has
all of the documents required under this Act for the clearance.

### [Voyage with a Person on Board without Their Consent]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53037 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** No master shall proceed
on a voyage with a person on board who is exercising powers or
performing duties relating to the administration or enforcement of a
relevant provision unless the person consents.

### [Reporting of Alleged Contraventions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53040 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable grounds

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual who has reasonable grounds to believe that a person or
  vessel has contravened or intends to contravene a relevant provision
  may notify the Minister of the particulars of the matter and may
  request that their identity be kept confidential with respect to the
  notification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The identity of an individual to whom the Minister
  has provided an assurance of confidentiality may be disclosed by the
  Minister only in accordance with the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  being notified under subsection 216(1), the Minister is to determine
  whether an inspection should be carried out by a marine safety
  inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a marine safety inspector determines that the
  individual who notified the Minister did not have reasonable grounds
  to believe that a person or vessel had contravened or had intended to
  contravene a relevant provision, the individual is liable to pay the
  costs of the inspection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  employer shall dismiss, suspend, demote, discipline, harass or
  otherwise disadvantage an employee, or deny an employee a benefit of
  employment, by reason that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, disclosed to the Minister that the employer or
    any other person had contravened or had intended to contravene a
    relevant provision;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, refused or stated an intention of refusing to
    do anything that is a contravention of a relevant provision;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, did or stated an intention of doing anything
    that is required to be done in order that a contravention of a
    relevant provision not be committed; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the employer believes that the employee will do
    anything referred to in paragraph (a), (b) or (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section impairs any right of an
  employee either at law or under an employment contract or collective
  agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  includes an independent contractor and
  [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has a corresponding meaning.

### [Investigations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53062 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigations

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may appoint any person to investigate a shipping casualty or
  an alleged contravention of a relevant provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person appointed under subsection (1) may not make
  findings as to the causes and contributing factors of a shipping
  casualty that has been or is being investigated by the Canadian
  Transportation Accident Investigation and Safety Board or that that
  Board has informed the Minister they propose to investigate.

- Definition of [[shipping casualty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[shipping
  casualty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any accident or incident associated with a vessel;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any situation or condition that the Minister has
    reasonable grounds to believe could, if left unattended, induce an
    accident or incident described in paragraph (a).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search and seizure without warrant

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the conditions for obtaining a warrant under section 487 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) exist in respect of the contravention
  of a relevant provision but by reason of exigent circumstances it
  would not be feasible to obtain one, a marine safety inspector may
  exercise the powers of search and seizure provided in that section
  without a warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Living quarters

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A marine safety inspector may not search living
  quarters without a warrant unless the inspector first obtains the
  consent of the occupant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional powers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the course of a search under subsection (1) or
  section 487 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), a marine safety
  inspector has all the powers referred to in subsection 211(4)
  (inspections).

### [Analysis and Examination]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53079 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  marine safety inspector may submit to a person designated by the
  Minister, for analysis or examination, anything seized or any sample
  taken under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate or report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who has made an analysis or examination may
  issue a certificate or report that sets out the results of the
  analysis or examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) and (5), the certificate
  or report is admissible in evidence in any proceeding related to the
  contravention of a relevant provision and, in the absence of any
  evidence to the contrary, is proof of the statements contained in the
  certificate or report without proof of the signature or the official
  character of the person appearing to have signed it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance of analyst

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The party against whom the certificate or report is
  produced may, with leave of the court, require for the purposes of
  cross-examination the attendance of the person who issued it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The certificate or report may be admitted in evidence
  only if the party who intends to produce it has given to the party
  against whom it is intended to be produced reasonable notice of that
  intention, together with a copy of the certificate or report.

### [Detention of Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53091 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Optional detention

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  marine safety inspector believes on reasonable grounds that a
  contravention of a relevant provision has been committed by or in
  respect of a vessel or that the vessel is not seaworthy, the inspector
  may make a detention order in respect of the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory detention --- unsafe vessels

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The inspector must make a detention order if the
  contravention is a contravention of section 110 (too many passengers)
  or the inspector also believes on reasonable grounds that the vessel
  is unsafe, that it is unfit to carry passengers or crew members or
  that its machinery or equipment is defective in any way so as to
  expose persons on board to serious danger.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory detention --- foreign vessels

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an information has been laid, an indictment has
  been preferred or a notice of violation has been issued or an
  assurance of compliance has been entered into under section 229 in
  respect of a contravention of a relevant provision that is alleged to
  have been committed by or in respect of a foreign vessel, a marine
  safety inspector must make a detention order in respect of the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be in writing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A detention order made under this section must be in
  writing and be addressed to every person empowered to grant clearance
  in respect of the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention order to be served on master

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Notice of a detention order made under this section
  in respect of a vessel must be served on the master

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by delivering a copy of the notice personally to
    the master; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if service cannot reasonably be effected in the
    manner provided in paragraph (a), by leaving a copy of the notice
    with the person who is, or appears to be, in charge of the vessel
    or, if there is no such person, by fixing a copy of the notice to a
    prominent part of the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The notice must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} indicate the measures to ensure compliance with the
    relevant provision or to render the vessel seaworthy that must be
    taken for the detention order to be rescinded;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign vessel, if an information
    has been laid, an indictment has been preferred or a notice of
    violation has been issued or an assurance of compliance has been
    entered into under section 229 in respect of a contravention of a
    relevant provision, indicate the amount and form of security that,
    pending the outcome of any proceedings related to the information,
    indictment or notice, must be deposited with the Minister for the
    detention order to be rescinded; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Canadian vessel, if an indictment
    has been preferred in respect of a contravention of a relevant
    provision, indicate the amount and form of security that, pending
    the outcome of any proceedings related to the indictment, must be
    deposited with the Minister for the detention order to be rescinded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign state to be notified

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel in respect of which a detention order is
  made under this section is registered in a foreign state, that state
  is to be notified that the order was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rescission of orders

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A marine safety inspector must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} rescind a detention order made under this section
    if the inspector is satisfied that the measures indicated in the
    notice referred to in subsection (6) have been taken and, if
    applicable, security in the amount and form indicated in the notice
    referred to in that subsection has been deposited with the Minister;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify, in the form and manner specified by the
    Minister, the master and the persons referred to in subsection (4)
    of the rescission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of persons empowered to give
  clearance

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} No person to whom a detention order made under this
  section is addressed shall, after notice of the order is received by
  them, grant clearance to the vessel in respect of which the order was
  made unless they have been notified that the order has been rescinded
  under subsection (8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Movement of vessel prohibited

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 224, no person shall move a
  vessel that is subject to a detention order made under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for expenses

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative and the owner of a
  vessel that is detained under this section are jointly and severally,
  or solidarily, liable for all expenses incurred in respect of the
  detained vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of security

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} The Minister, after proceedings in respect of which
  security was deposited are concluded,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} may apply the security to reimburse Her Majesty in
    right of Canada, either fully or partially, if any of the expenses
    or any fine or penalty has not been paid; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is to return the security, or any part of it that
    remains if it is applied under paragraph (a), if all expenses and
    any fine or penalty imposed have been paid.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 222
- [2023, c. 26, s. 413](#1410476-1413982){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410476-1413982.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interference with service

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
wilfully interfere with the service of a notice of a detention order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction to move a detained vessel

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on application made by the authorized representative
  of a detained vessel in the form and manner specified by the Minister,
  permit the master to move it in accordance with the directions of the
  Minister;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on application made by the owner of a dock or wharf
  or by the person in charge of a harbour at which a detained vessel is
  situated in the form and manner specified by the Minister, direct the
  person who is, or appears to be, in charge of the vessel to move the
  vessel in accordance with the directions of the Minister; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a person to whom a direction is given under
  paragraph (b) does not comply with it and the Minister is satisfied
  that the applicant for the direction has sufficient insurance in place
  to cover any incident that may arise from the moving of the vessel,
  authorize the applicant to move the vessel in accordance with the
  Minister's directions and at the expense of the authorized
  representative and the owner of the vessel, for which the authorized
  representative and the owner are jointly and severally, or solidarily,
  liable for the expense of moving the vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 224
- [2023, c. 26, s. 414](#1410478-1413986){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410478-1413986.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-224-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 224"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Sale of Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53133 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distress on vessel for sums ordered to be
paid

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** A court, justice of the
peace or provincial court judge that orders the authorized
representative of a vessel to pay any fine, crew member's wages or other
sum of money may, if payment is not made in accordance with the order,
order the seizure and sale of the vessel or its machinery or equipment.

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 1, s.
  148\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessel may be seized and sold if fine
  or penalty not paid

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At any time after a fine is imposed under a relevant
  provision against, or a certificate is registered in the Federal Court
  under subsection 235(2) in respect of, a vessel, its authorized
  representative or its owner, the Minister may, while the fine or debt
  remains unpaid, seize the vessel and, after giving notice to the
  authorized representative or owner, sell it and, by bill of sale, give
  the purchaser a valid title to the vessel free from any mortgage or
  other claim on the vessel that exists at the time of the sale.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceeds of sale

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any surplus remaining from the proceeds of a sale is
  to be distributed in accordance with the regulations after satisfying
  the following claims in the following order:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the cost of the seizure and sale;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any claim for salvage in respect of the vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the claims of masters and crew members for wages;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the claims of masters for disbursements made or
    liabilities incurred for necessaries on account of the vessel;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the amount of any fine imposed or debt due under a
    relevant provision; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the cost of returning masters and crew members to
    the place where they first came on board or to another place to
    which they and the Minister have agreed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If proceeds insufficient

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the proceeds of sale of a vessel are insufficient
  to satisfy the claims described in subsection (3), the Minister may
  proceed for the balance owing against

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative and the owner,
    jointly and severally, or solidarily, in the case of a Canadian
    vessel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the owner, in the case of a foreign vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 226
- [2019, c. 1, s. 148](#1114290-1196293){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1114290-1196293.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 26, s. 415](#1410480-1413990){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410480-1413990.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-226-20190730.html "Link to previous version of section 226"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Foreign Vessels in Contravention of International Conventions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53153 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's powers

- **[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister has reasonable grounds to believe that a foreign vessel
  is in contravention of an international convention, protocol or
  resolution listed in Schedule 1, the Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel has not entered Canadian waters,
    direct the vessel not to enter Canadian waters;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel has entered Canadian waters but is
    not in a port in Canada, direct the vessel to leave Canadian waters;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is in a port in Canada, direct the
    vessel to leave Canadian waters, subject to any terms and conditions
    that the Minister may specify.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may not give a direction under
  subsection (1) if, in the Minister's opinion, doing so would put the
  safety of the vessel, any person on board or the environment at
  imminent risk.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister gives a direction under
  subsection (1) in respect of a vessel, the Minister must notify the
  state where the vessel is registered of the direction given and the
  reason for it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 227
- [2023, c. 26, s. 416](#1410485-1413994){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410485-1413994.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 227"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Administrative Penalties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53164 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-53165 .Subheading}

Definition of [[violation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** In sections 229 to 244,
[[violation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a contravention of a relevant
provision, or a contravention of a direction given under a relevant
provision, that is designated as a violation by the regulations made
under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 228
- 2014, c. 29, s. 74
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-228-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 228"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Assurances of Compliance and Notices of Violation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-53170 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If reasonable grounds to believe a
violation

- **[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister has reasonable grounds to believe that a person or vessel
  has committed a violation, the Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter into an assurance of compliance with the
    person or vessel that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} identifies the violation and provides that the
      person or vessel will comply with the provision to which the
      violation relates within the period, and be subject to the terms
      and conditions, specified in the assurance,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} sets out the amount and form of any security
      that, pending compliance with the assurance, must be deposited
      with the Minister, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} sets out the penalty, fixed by or within the
      range fixed by the regulations made under this Part, for the
      violation that the person or vessel would have been liable to pay
      if the assurance had not been entered into; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issue, and cause to be served on the person or
    vessel, a notice of violation that names the person or vessel,
    identifies the violation and sets out

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the penalty, fixed by or within the range fixed
      by the regulations made under this Part, for the violation that
      the person or vessel is liable to pay,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period, being thirty days after the notice
      is served, within which the penalty must be paid or a review of
      the notice requested, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} particulars of the manner in which, and the
      address at which, the penalty must be paid or a review requested.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may extend the period specified under
  subparagraph (1)(a)(i) if the Minister is satisfied that the person
  with whom or vessel with which the assurance of compliance was entered
  into is unable to comply with it because of reasons beyond the
  person's or vessel's control.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short-form descriptions in notices of
  violation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may establish, in respect of each
  violation, a short-form description to be used in notices of
  violation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 229, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-229-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 229"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed violation

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who, or vessel that, enters into an assurance of compliance is,
  unless a review is requested under subsection (2), deemed to have
  committed the violation in respect of which the assurance was entered
  into.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who, or vessel that, enters into an
  assurance of compliance may, within 48 hours after the assurance is
  signed, unless a notice of default is served within that period under
  section 231.1, request a review of the facts of the violation, in
  which case the assurance is deemed to be a notice of violation and a
  review under paragraph 232(1)(b) of the facts of the violation and the
  amount of the penalty is deemed to have been requested.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 230, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-230-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 230"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When assurance of compliance complied
with

**[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** If the Minister is
satisfied that an assurance of compliance has been complied with, the
Minister must cause a notice to that effect to be served on the person
or vessel and, on the service of the notice,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} no further proceedings may be taken against the
  person or vessel with respect to the violation in respect of which the
  assurance was entered into; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any security deposited under subparagraph
  229(1)(a)(ii) must be returned to the person or vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 231, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-231-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 231"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When assurance of compliance not complied
with

- **[[231.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion
  that a person who, or vessel that, has entered into an assurance of
  compliance has not complied with it, the Minister may cause a notice
  of default to be served on the person or vessel to the effect that,
  unless a member determines under section 231.2, or an appeal panel
  decides under section 232.2, that the assurance has been complied
  with,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person or vessel is liable to pay double the
    amount of the penalty set out in the assurance; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the security deposited under subparagraph
    229(1)(a)(ii) is forfeited to Her Majesty in right of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice under subsection (1) must include the
  address at which, and the date, being 30 days after the notice is
  served, on or before which, a request for a review may be filed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No set-off

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On the service of a notice of default, the person or
  vessel served has no right of set-off against any amount spent by the
  person or vessel under the assurance of compliance.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

- **[[231.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person or vessel served with a
  notice under subsection 231.1(1) may, on or before the date specified
  in the notice or within any further time that the Tribunal on
  application allows, file a written request for a review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and place for review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a request filed under subsection (1),
  the Tribunal must appoint a time and place for the review and must
  notify the Minister and the person who, or vessel that, filed the
  request of the time and place in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review procedure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The member of the Tribunal assigned to conduct the
  review must provide the Minister and the person or vessel with an
  opportunity consistent with procedural fairness and natural justice to
  present evidence and make representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The burden is on the Minister to establish that the
  person or vessel did not comply with the assurance of compliance
  referred to in the notice. The person is not required, and must not be
  compelled, to give any evidence or testimony in the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain defences not available

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person or vessel named in a notice of default does
  not have a defence by reason that the person or vessel exercised due
  diligence to comply with the assurance of compliance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The member may confirm the Minister's decision or
  determine that the person or vessel has complied with the assurance of
  compliance.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of security

**[[231.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231.3 .sectionLabel}** Any security
deposited under subparagraph 229(1)(a)(ii) must be returned to the
person or vessel if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person or vessel pays double the amount of the
  penalty set out in the assurance of compliance in accordance with a
  notice of default under section 231.1; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a member determines under section 231.2, or an appeal
  panel decides under section 232.2, that the assurance of compliance
  has been complied with.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of violation

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person or vessel served with a notice of violation under paragraph
  229(1)(b) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pay the amount of the penalty; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within thirty days after being served or any
    further time that the Tribunal on application allows, file with the
    Tribunal a written request for a review of the facts of the
    violation or the amount of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When review not requested

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a review of the facts of the violation is not
  requested, the person or vessel is deemed to have committed the
  violation in respect of which the notice was served.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 232, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-232-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 232"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and place for review

- **[[232.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a request filed under
  paragraph 232(1)(b), the Tribunal must appoint a time and place for
  the review and must notify the Minister and the person who, or vessel
  that, filed the request of the time and place in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The member of the Tribunal assigned to conduct the
  review must provide the Minister and the person or vessel with an
  opportunity consistent with procedural fairness and natural justice to
  present evidence and make representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden is on the Minister to establish that the
  person or vessel committed the violation referred to in the notice.
  The person is not required, and must not be compelled, to give any
  evidence or testimony in the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The member may confirm the Minister's decision or,
  subject to any regulations made under paragraph 244(h), substitute his
  or her own determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal

- **[[232.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister or the person who, or
  vessel that, requested the review may, within 30 days after the
  determination, appeal a determination made under subsection 231.2(6)
  or 232.1(4) to the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of right of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party that does not appear at a review hearing is
  not entitled to appeal a determination, unless they establish that
  there was sufficient reason to justify their absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The appeal panel of the Tribunal assigned to hear the
  appeal may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination made under
    subsection 231.2(6), dismiss the appeal or allow the appeal and
    substitute its own decision; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination made under
    subsection 232.1(4), dismiss the appeal, or allow the appeal and,
    subject to regulations made under paragraph 244(h), substitute its
    own decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 29, s. 72
:::

#### [Choice of Proceedings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-53264 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How contravention may be proceeded with

**[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** If a contravention can
be proceeded with as a violation or as an offence, the Minister may
commence proceedings in respect of that contravention as a violation or
recommend that it be proceeded with as an offence, but it may be
proceeded with only as one or the other.

#### [Recovery of Debts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-53267 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts due to Her Majesty

**[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** The following amounts
constitute debts due to Her Majesty in right of Canada that may be
recovered in a court of competent jurisdiction:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} unless a review of the amount of the penalty is
  requested under paragraph 232(1)(b), the amount of a penalty set out
  in a notice of violation, from the time the notice is served;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount that a notice of default served under
  subsection 231.1(1) provides that a person is liable to pay, from the
  time the notice is served;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount of a penalty determined by a member after
  a review under section 232.1, or decided by an appeal panel after an
  appeal under section 232.2, from the time of the respective
  determination or decision; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the amount of any reasonable expenses incurred in
  attempting to recover an amount referred to in any of paragraphs (a)
  to (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 234, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-234-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 234"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

- **[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  or part of a debt referred to in section 234 in respect of which there
  is a default of payment may be certified by the Minister or the
  Tribunal, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judgments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On production to the Federal Court, a certificate
  made under subsection (1) is to be registered in that Court and, when
  registered, has the same force and effect, and all proceedings may be
  taken on it, as if it were a judgment obtained in that Court for a
  debt of the amount specified in it and all reasonable costs and
  charges attendant in its registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 235, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-235-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 235"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Rules of Law about Violations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-53283 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violations are not offences

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
a violation is not an offence and, accordingly, section 126 of the
[Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) does not apply.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Common law principles

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** Every rule and
principle of the common law that renders any circumstance a
justification or an excuse in relation to a charge for an offence under
a relevant provision applies in respect of a violation to the extent
that it is not inconsistent with this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vicarious liability --- Canadian maritime
document holders

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a Canadian maritime document is liable for a violation that
  is committed in respect of any matter that relates to an activity or a
  requirement under that document, whether or not the person or vessel
  that actually committed the violation is identified or proceeded
  against in accordance with this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vicarious liability --- acts of
  employees and agents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person or vessel is liable for a violation that is
  committed by an employee or agent of the person or vessel acting in
  the course of the employee's employment or within the scope of the
  agent's authority, whether or not the employee or agent who actually
  committed the violation is identified or proceeded against in
  accordance with this Act.

#### [General Provisions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-53293 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notations removed

- **[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Minister is of the
  opinion that it is not in the public interest to do so, five years
  after the last day on which any of the following events occurs the
  Minister is to remove every notation of violation or default, and
  every suspension or cancellation of or refusal to issue or renew a
  Canadian maritime document on prescribed grounds, from any records
  that the Minister may keep respecting a person or vessel:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person or vessel pays every penalty that the
    person or vessel is liable to pay after a notice of violation, a
    notice of default, a determination of a member of the Tribunal on
    review or a decision of an appeal panel of the Tribunal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a suspension on prescribed grounds of a Canadian
    maritime document issued to the person or vessel ends;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister cancels, on prescribed grounds, a
    Canadian maritime document issued to the person or vessel; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister refuses, on prescribed grounds, to
    issue a Canadian maritime document to the person or vessel or to
    renew a Canadian maritime document issued to the person or vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to notify

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister is to give a person or vessel notice of
  a decision not to remove a notation in respect of the person or vessel
  and providing all relevant information concerning the grounds on which
  the Minister has refused to remove it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A notice under subsection (2) must include the
  address at which, and the date, being thirty days after the notice is
  served, on or before which, a request for a review may be filed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person or vessel may, on or before the date
  specified in the notice or within any further time that the Tribunal
  on application allows, file a written request for a review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and place for review

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a request filed under subsection (4),
  the Tribunal must appoint a time and place for the review and must
  notify the Minister and the person who, or vessel that, filed the
  request of the time and place in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review procedure

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The member of the Tribunal assigned to conduct the
  review must provide the Minister and the person who, or vessel that,
  filed the request with an opportunity consistent with procedural
  fairness and natural justice to present evidence and make
  representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The member may confirm the Minister's decision or
  refer the matter back to the Minister for reconsideration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The person who, or vessel that, requested the review
  may, within 30 days after the determination, appeal a determination
  made under subsection (7) to the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of right of appeal

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the person or vessel does not appear at the review
  hearing, the person or vessel is not entitled to appeal a
  determination unless the person or vessel establishes that there was
  sufficient reason to justify the absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The appeal panel assigned to hear the appeal may
  dismiss the appeal or refer the matter back to the Minister for
  reconsideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 239, c. 29, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-239-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 239"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of notations of violations

**[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall keep
a public record of notations of violations or default kept on any record
that the Minister may keep respecting the person or vessel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 240
- [2023, c. 26, s. 417(F)](#1410487-1413997){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410487-1413997.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 240"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** No notice of violation
may be issued more than two years after the day on which the Minister
becomes aware of the contravention.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of Minister

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** A document that
purports to have been issued by the Minister and certifies the day on
which the Minister became aware of a violation is evidence, without
proof of the signature or official character of the person appearing to
have signed the document, that the Minister became aware of the
violation on that day.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When compliance agreement in effect

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** If a violation arising
out of the contravention of any provision of Part 4, 8 or 9 or the
regulations made under those Parts is committed while an agreement or
arrangement is in effect between the Minister and the authorized
representative of a Canadian vessel that provides that inspections of
the vessel to ensure compliance with that provision will be carried out
by the authorized representative or a person or an organization acting
on their behalf, the penalty or range of penalties fixed under paragraph
244(h) in respect of the violation is doubled.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 243
- 2018, c. 27, s. 708
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-243-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 243"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53331 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for
carrying out the purposes and provisions of this Part, including
regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting clearances for departure from a port in
  Canada;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the investigation of shipping casualties,
  the alleged contravention of a relevant provision or an incident that
  involved a vessel and that, in the opinion of the Minister,
  jeopardized the safety of persons;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the detention of vessels, including the
  review of detention orders;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 1, s. 149\]]{.Repealed}

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the sale of a vessel under section 226,
  including measures that must be taken before the vessel is sold and
  the manner of establishing, and the priority of payment of, claims
  against the vessel;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} designating as a violation that may be proceeded with
  in accordance with sections 229 to 242 the contravention of a relevant
  provision, or the contravention of a direction given under a relevant
  provision, that is an offence under this Act;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} designating violations that, if continued on more
  than one day, constitute a separate violation for each day on which
  they are continued;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} designating violations that may be proceeded with by
  issuing notices of violation and fixing a penalty or a range of
  penalties in respect of each such violation, up to a maximum of
  \$250,000 but in any event not greater than the maximum fine if the
  violation were proceeded with by way of summary conviction;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} respecting emergency and safety procedures for the
  purpose of paragraph 211(4)(d.1);

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} respecting the service of documents required or
  authorized to be served under this Part, including the manner of
  serving them, the proof of their service and the circumstances under
  which they are deemed to have been served; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that by this Part is to be
  prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 244, c. 29, s. 72
- 2014, c. 29, s. 75
- 2018, c. 27, s. 709
- [2019, c. 1, s. 149](#1114291-1196296){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1114291-1196296.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-244-20181213.html "Link to previous version of section 244"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53347 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of Act

- **[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, or vessel that,
  contravenes any of the following commits an offence:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under subsection 211(3) (to stop
    a vessel or proceed as specified);

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under subsection 211(3.1)
    (direction to authorize a vessel);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under any of paragraphs 211(4)(a)
    to (e) (to answer reasonable questions, to provide reasonable
    assistance, to prohibit or limit access to part of a vessel, to
    operate or cease operating machinery or equipment, to not move a
    vessel, to muster crew or carry out emergency or safety procedures
    or to produce documents);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} section 215 (proceed to sea with a person without
    their consent);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subsection 218(1) (disciplining employee);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} subsection 222(9) (giving clearance to detained
    vessel);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} subsection 222(10) (moving detained vessel);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} section 223 (wilfully interfering with service of
    notice); or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under subsection 227(1) (not to
    enter or to leave Canadian waters).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, or vessel that, commits an offence
  under subsection (1) is liable on summary conviction to a fine of not
  more than \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than
  18 months, or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 245
- [2023, c. 26, s. 418](#1410489-1414004){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410489-1414004.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-245-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 245"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of Act or regulations

- **[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, or vessel that,
  contravenes any of the following commits an offence:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a direction given under subsection 212(2) (to store
    something);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} section 213 (departing without clearance); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a provision of the regulations made under this
    Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, or vessel that, commits an offence
  under subsection (1) is liable on summary conviction to a fine of not
  more than \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than one
  year, or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 26, s. 246
- 2015, c. 3, s. 25(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-246-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 246"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 12]{.HLabel1}[Miscellaneous]{.HTitleText1} {#h-53371 .Part}

### [Goods]{.HTitleText2} {#h-53372 .Subheading}

Definition of [[carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** In sections 248 and
250, [[carrier]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person with whom a shipper
of goods enters into a contract of carriage of the goods by water.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Carrier's lien

- **[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  carrier has, on goods carried under a contract of carriage, a lien for
  any amount that is due to the carrier under the contract for freight
  or costs of storing, disposing of or preserving the goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of lien to owner of goods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before exercising a lien, a carrier must give notice
  of it to the owner of the goods, specifying the amount claimed and the
  particulars of the claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of lien to third parties

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the carrier places the goods in the custody of a
  third party and gives the third party notice of the lien and the third
  party does not retain them until notified by the carrier that the lien
  is discharged or does not return them to the carrier, the third party
  is liable to the carrier. The third party may charge the carrier for
  storing the goods or for any reasonable action taken to preserve them
  or to protect property or human life from damage they may cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Third party's protection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A third party who retains goods until notified by the
  carrier that the lien is discharged or who returns them to the carrier
